# Decisions

Record of Navigator decisions and Opus rejections. Only add to this file. Each entry has the date, the unit, the decision and the reason.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — batch and step list (iteration 1)

- **Decision:** Batch `[PLT-01]` approved. Step list accepted: write `plans/PLT-01.md` with `status: planned`, commit it locally, do not push. Implementation is a later iteration.
- **Reason:** PLT-01 is the only unblocked unit in the first open phase (Platform `phase:scaffold`). No plan exists yet. No git remote is configured, so the work stays local.
- **Correction:** The Navigator's carry-forward line "no DECISIONS.md entry is forced this iteration" is wrong. The driver rules (steps 2 and 5) require each Navigator decision to be recorded here, so this entry is required.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — how the Angular, NestJS and Zod majors are pinned (Option B)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option B. PLT-01 records the Angular, NestJS and Zod majors in a documented pin section in the root `package.json`, and installs none of the three packages. The resolved numbers are recorded when PLT-01 is implemented, not hardcoded in the plan. PLT-02 adds `zod` to `packages/contracts`, PLT-03 adds `@angular/*` and Material to `apps/web`, and PLT-04 adds `@nestjs/core` and `@nestjs/serve-static` to `apps/api`, each at its recorded major. The pin is a section, not npm `overrides`.
- **Reason (Navigator):** Keeps the three packages out of the lockfile until their owning units. Avoids baking possibly stale version numbers into a plan that is not implemented in this iteration.
- **Driver challenge (open at the time of writing):** The stated reason "no downstream file changes" does not separate Option A from Option B. Under both, PLT-02, PLT-03 and PLT-04 edit the workspace `package.json` files that PLT-01 creates (PLT-01 Scope line 32). No acceptance criterion tests the pin section (AC 4 covers only Node and npm). The challenge is recorded in a separate navigator question. Its answer will be added here.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — answer to the challenge on Option B (criterion added)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option (a). Keep Option B. The reason is corrected to the ownership boundary: PLT-01 is the scaffold and must not pre-declare dependencies owned by PLT-02 (contracts), PLT-03 (web) and PLT-04 (api). PLT-01 records the three majors so those units install at the recorded major. Option A (install now) is rejected.
- **Criterion change (Navigator, PLT-01):** Added one acceptance criterion: the root `package.json` pin section names the Angular, NestJS and Zod majors. A test asserts that the three names are present. Version numbers are not checked. This is additive; no existing criterion was removed or weakened. The unit file records the change.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The stated reason "no downstream file changes" did not separate Option A from Option B, so it was withdrawn. The pin is required by PLT-01's Decisions ("Angular, NestJS and Zod majors are pinned here"). No criterion tested it, so a criterion is added. A presence-only check avoids baking stale version numbers into the plan.
- **Driver note:** The challenge recorded in the entry above is closed by this answer.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — TypeScript major in the pin section

- **Decision (Navigator, confirmed):** TypeScript is pinned to the 6.0.x line (range `>=6.0 <6.1`). The exact patch is resolved when PLT-01 is implemented and recorded in the root `package.json` pin section. 7.x and 5.x are rejected. The earlier "6.x" label is withdrawn as too wide.
- **Reason (Navigator, checked by the driver against the npm registry, 2026-11-10):** `@angular/build@22.2.5`, `@angular-devkit/build-angular@22.2.5` and `@angular/compiler-cli@22.2.5` each declare peer `typescript` `>=6.0 <6.1`, which alone forces 6.0.x. The registry `latest` is 7.0.2, which those packages exclude. Angular 21 accepts `>=5.9 <6.0` and Angular 20 accepts `>=5.8 <6.0`, so no Angular line checked accepts 7.x. `typescript-eslint` (latest) declares `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`, a secondary constraint not required by the PLT-01 unit.
- **Why the newest-major rule does not apply to TypeScript:** the rule was set for Angular, NestJS and Zod. Applied to TypeScript it picks 7.0.2, which the Angular build tooling excludes.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Node runtime for the tests

- **Decision (Navigator):** The Node pin is Node 26 in `.nvmrc` and `engines`, as PLT-01 Decisions line 25 requires. This iteration writes `plans/PLT-01.md` with `status: planned` only and runs no tests. No local Node 24.21.0 run is recorded, and no criterion is marked passed. The four acceptance-criterion tests run on a Node 26 runtime at implementation time, obtained from nodejs.org or the npm `node@26` package.
- **Reason (Navigator):** A Node 24 run would misrepresent the pin, and no test runs in this iteration anyway. Changing the pin to Node 24 would alter the unit's stated decision and force a unit edit.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — location and coverage of the pin-section test

- **Decision (Navigator):** The test for the new pin-section criterion is `scripts/pins.test.mjs` at the repo root. It runs with Node's built-in `node:test`, invoked by the root `test` script after the workspace fan-out. It asserts that the keys `angular`, `nestjs` and `zod` are present in the root pin section. It does not check version numbers, and it has no TypeScript key.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The pin section is in the root `package.json`, so the test is root-owned. `packages/contracts` belongs to PLT-02, so the test is not placed there. A root Vitest config would add a file and a dependency for a presence check. `node:test` is built into Node, and the root `test` script already fans out to every workspace. The criterion names only the three majors, so the coverage matches it.
- **Correction:** The Navigator's reason "a TypeScript key would force version checking" is wrong. A presence check on a TypeScript key does not check its version. The coverage choice still stands, because the criterion names only the three majors, and widening the criterion is the Navigator's call.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Opus rejection 1 of the plan (blocking problems)

- **Rejection (Opus subagent, plan review):** `plans/PLT-01.md` is not approved. Count for PLT-01: 1 rejection.
- **Blocking 1:** Root `prettier --check .` (plan section 2) also checks the tracked Markdown files in `units/`, `modules/` and `plans/`, and the plan adds no `.prettierignore`. So AC 2 cannot pass. The driver confirmed this: Prettier 3 flags `units/PLT-01-workspace-scaffold.md` with default config.
- **Blocking 2:** The AC 3 command `find node_modules -path '*/node_modules/typescript/package.json'` returns 0 for the root copy, because the path has no `/` before its first `node_modules`. The driver confirmed this: the plan's pattern returns 0 and `find . -path '*/node_modules/typescript/package.json'` returns 1.
- **Non-blocking notes to fold into the plan:** the workspace `lint` runs Prettier, which reads only the cwd's ignore files, so it can check generated `dist/` output; the section 5 downstream list misses PLT-12's possible `e2e/` workspace, PLT-04's `build` and `tsconfig` changes, PLT-03's deletion of the placeholder test, and PLT-11's `concurrently` and `.scriba-dev/` use; pins must be resolved before the first install; the ESLint version wording should not name a version; the `node -v` and `npm -v` output should be recorded with each result; the pin section should be documented with a `//` key.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Prettier scope (Navigator decision, after Opus rejection 1)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option (a). A root `.prettierignore` excludes `units/`, `modules/` and `plans/`. Prettier then checks code and config in the four workspaces only. The driver fixes to the plan are accepted: the AC 3 `find` pattern (`find . -path '*/node_modules/typescript/package.json'`), workspace `lint` as ESLint only, the completed section 5 downstream list, pins resolved before the first install, version-free ESLint wording, node and npm versions recorded with each result, and `scribaPins` documented with a `//` key.
- **Reason (Navigator):** It honors the unit's decision "ESLint flat config plus Prettier check across workspaces" (PLT-01 line 26) without a unit edit, because the document folders are not workspaces. It is additive. It avoids reformatting about 212 documents (option b), and it avoids the unit edit that dropping Prettier would need (option c).

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Opus rejection 2 of the plan (blocking problem)

- **Rejection (Opus subagent, re-review of revision 2):** `plans/PLT-01.md` is not approved. Count for PLT-01: 2 rejections. The next rejection is the third, and at three the Navigator must choose to make the unit smaller, divide it, or stop it.
- **Blocking:** The `.prettierignore` entries in revision 2 are unanchored (`modules/`). Gitignore rules make such a pattern match a folder of that name at any depth, so `apps/api/src/modules/**` would be ignored too. The driver confirmed this with Prettier 3: `apps/api/src/modules/platform/platform.module.ts` reports `"ignored": true` with `modules/`, and `"ignored": false` with `/modules/`.
- **Non-blocking, folded into the revision:** PLT-03's replacement `package.json` must keep `build`, `test` and `lint` scripts. PLT-09's fixtures must fail lint, so the root config needs an ignore for their folder. PLT-13 also adds a root `e2e` script. PLT-11 adds a root `README.md` that Prettier checks. Prettier formatting is applied to `package.json` before the check. The unit branch `unit/PLT-01` is created before the first commit, and the fresh clone uses `-b unit/PLT-01`. The chosen Angular major's build peer must admit the 6.0 patch. The `packageManager` value must match an exact npm version.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Opus rejection 3 of the plan (blocking problem)

- **Rejection (Opus subagent, round 3 review of revision 3):** `plans/PLT-01.md` is not approved. Count for PLT-01: 3 rejections. Per the driver rules, at three rejections the Navigator must select one of: make the unit smaller, divide the unit, or stop the unit. No further plan revision is made before that selection.
- **Blocking:** The workspace `package.json` files have no `version`. npm prints the package name in each workspace's run banner only when `version` is set. The driver confirmed this in a temporary two-workspace project: the workspace without `version` printed `> build`, and the one with `"version": "0.0.0"` printed `> @x/b@0.0.0 build`. So AC 2's requirement that each fan-out name all four packages cannot be met as written. The fix is `"version": "0.0.0"` in each workspace, plus a banner check in AC 2.
- **Non-blocking, to fold in if the unit continues:** Step 1 must resolve the tool versions and require `npm install` to succeed without `--legacy-peer-deps`. Step 1c must check the exact Angular version from step 1a, and also the `@angular/compiler-cli` peer. Step 3 must run AC 2 to 5 in the working tree, with AC 1 run after the step 4 commit. Step 2 must run `prettier --write` after the install, so the locked Prettier version formats the files. Section 5 must cover the TypeScript devDependency that the Angular and NestJS generators add, the root Prettier check reaching the files PLT-03, PLT-10 and PLT-12 add, PLT-12's tests not running under root `npm test` if `e2e/` stays a plain folder, and any root devDependency PLT-09 adds. The smoke test must import `describe`, `it` and `expect` from `vitest`.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — response to the third rejection (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Keep PLT-01 as one unit and fix the defects in place. No scope cut, no division, no stop. Apply the blocking fix (`"version": "0.0.0"` in each of the four workspace `package.json` files, plus a banner check that npm names all four packages in AC 2) and the non-blocking notes above. Then re-send the plan to Opus.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The three rejections are mechanical defects, not scope problems. Dividing would change the `blocked_by` and `unblocks` fields of about ten units and the README phase table for a one-line version field. Stopping would block the whole build.
- **Driver note on the label:** The question offered "(a) make the unit smaller" as the first option. The Navigator's answer is labelled (a), but its text describes no scope cut. The driver records the selection as keep-whole, fix-in-place. This is not one of the three literal options. It is flagged here so the user can see it.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — Angular and TypeScript conflict rule (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option (a). The newest-Angular-major rule and the TypeScript 6.0.x line are two separate Navigator decisions. If the newest Angular major's build-tooling TypeScript peer excludes the 6.0.x line, the implementation stops and records the conflict for the Navigator. It does not pick a non-newest Angular major, and it does not move the TypeScript line.
- **Reason (Navigator):** Choosing an older Angular major would silently downgrade a decision. Moving the TypeScript line would silently reopen the 6.0.x decision, whose premise is the Angular 22.2.5 build peer. Only the Navigator may re-decide either one.

## 2026-11-10 — PLT-01 — versions resolved at implementation (plan section 3, steps 1a to 1e)

- **Resolved (driver, Node v26.13.0, npm 11.20.0):** Angular major 22 (`@angular/core` 22.2.5). NestJS major 12 (`@nestjs/core` 12.4.2). Zod major 4 (`zod` 4.6.7). TypeScript 6.0.3, the highest of `typescript@6.0` (`npm view typescript@6.0 version` lists 6.0.2 and 6.0.3). Tools: `vitest` 5.1.0, `eslint` 10.14.0, `@eslint/js` 10.0.1, `typescript-eslint` 8.72.2, `prettier` 3.9.10. `packageManager` is `npm@11.20.0`, the npm bundled with Node 26.13.0.
- **Peer checks (steps 1c and 1d):** `@angular/build@22.2.5` and `@angular/compiler-cli@22.2.5` both declare `typescript` `>=6.0 <6.1`, which admits 6.0.3. `@angular/build@22.2.5` declares `vitest` `^4.0.8 || ^5.0.0`, so the newest admitted vitest is 5.1.0. `typescript-eslint@8.72.2` declares `eslint` `^8.57.0 || ^9.0.0 || ^10.0.0` and `typescript` `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`, which admit eslint 10.14.0 and TypeScript 6.0.3. `@eslint/js@10.0.1` declares `eslint` `^10.0.0`.

## 2026-11-11 — PLT-02 — mismatch 2: build before test for the contracts package (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option A. The `packages/contracts` workspace owns a build-before-test step inside its own `test` script (a `pretest` hook, or a test script that runs the `tsc` build first). PLT-02's runtime boot test (criterion 1, `units/PLT-02-contracts-package-platform-slice.md` line 63) then loads the built JS and `.d.ts` output. The CI order in PLT-10 (npm ci → lint → test → build, criterion 2 at line 25 and Decisions line 18) stays unchanged. No criterion text changes. Note 7 (line 105 of this file) is assigned to PLT-02.
- **Rejected (Navigator):** Option B, which moves the build before the test in PLT-10's job. It would change a criterion pinned in two places, a one-way change. Option C, which runs the tests against TypeScript source through a path mapping, contradicts PLT-02's stated rationale (line 15: consumed as built JS and `.d.ts` without path-alias tricks) and weakens criterion 1.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The build step belongs to the package that needs it, and the CI order stays as the unit fixes it. Mismatch 1 already set the precedent of keeping PLT-10's criteria unchanged.
- **Driver note:** apps/api tests that import the built contracts package (note 7) depend on `packages/contracts` running its `pretest` first. The root test fan-out runs `packages/contracts` first, so this holds by workspace order. PLT-04's plan must state this dependency, not rely on it silently.
- **Driver note:** Recorded now, before the PLT-02 plan is drafted, because driver rule 5 requires each decision to be recorded and committed at once.

## 2026-11-11 — PLT-09 — mismatch 3: lint fixtures versus the root ESLint config (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option A. The PLT-09 lint-test fixture folder is added to the root ESLint `ignores` array (`eslint.config.mjs` line 7). `npm run lint`, and therefore PLT-10's CI lint step, stays green. The fixtures stay as `.ts` files, so their relative import paths stay valid. The PLT-09 test runs ESLint's Node API on each fixture with the ignore lifted (for example `new ESLint({ ignore: false, overrideConfig })` or `overrideConfigFile`), and asserts that the boundary errors appear.
- **Rejected (Navigator):** Option B, a non-`.ts` extension with a temporary copy per test, because its temporary copy is fragile. Option C, a second ESLint config for the test, because it adds a second file on top of the same ignore.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The root config's comment at line 4 anticipates later units adding entries. The ignore alone does not satisfy PLT-09 criterion 5 ("don't ship"). The PLT-09 plan must pin the fixture folder as a repo-root lint-test folder, outside `packages/` and `apps/`, and must state two mechanisms: the ESLint ignore, and the fact that the folder is not an npm workspace, so `npm run build --workspaces` never builds it.
- **No criterion text changes.** PLT-10's CI order (npm ci → lint → test → build) is untouched. Recorded as a plan note for PLT-09, not as a criterion change.
- **Driver note:** Recorded now, before the plans are drafted, because driver rule 5 requires each decision to be recorded and committed at once.

## 2026-11-11 — batch [PLT-02, PLT-03, PLT-09, PLT-10] — root test script: one quoted glob (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Replace the explicit file list in the root `test` script with a quoted Node-native glob, `node --test 'scripts/*.test.mjs'`. PLT-03 is the first unit by ID that edits the root `test` line, so it sets the glob in its plan. PLT-09 and PLT-10 (and PLT-12 later) add their own test file only and do not edit the `test` line. No runner script is added.
- **Change to an approved contract (recorded):** This changes the root `test` line that PLT-01 approved (DECISIONS 2026-11-10, "location and coverage of the pin-section test", which named one file). The glob is a superset: `scripts/pins.test.mjs` still runs. This is a one-way change to a PLT-01-approved line, and it is recorded here.
- **Reason (Navigator):** A single line shared by four units conflicts at every merge. With a glob, a new root test needs no `package.json` edit. A glob is simpler than a runner script, and Node's own glob is cross-platform.
- **Verified locally (driver, Node 24.21.0 at the time):** `node --test 'scripts/*.test.mjs'` and the shell-expanded form each ran every matching test file, reported 2 tests for 2 files, and did not run a non-test helper file. Node 26 behaviour is re-verified at implementation.
- **Driver note:** Recorded now, before the plans are revised, because driver rule 5 requires each decision to be recorded and committed at once.

## 2026-11-11 — PLT-02 — output format: CommonJS (Navigator decision, disposition of PLT-02 open point 1)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option A, CommonJS, as drafted. `packages/contracts/package.json` has no `"type": "module"`, and `tsconfig.base.json` keeps `module` and `moduleResolution` NodeNext, so tsc emits CJS. PLT-02 criterion 1 (`units/PLT-02-contracts-package-platform-slice.md` line 63) is tested with a direct CJS `require` from a NestJS-style consumer, as the criterion names it.
- **Consequence handed to PLT-11:** The Angular build may warn about a CommonJS dependency and may need an `allowedCommonJsDependencies` entry. PLT-11 owns the Angular half of the cross-runtime boot test (`units/PLT-11-scaffold-gate.md` lines 22 and 30), so it makes that decision.
- **Rejected (Navigator):** Option B, ESM with `"type": "module"`. It would make NestJS, which is CJS by default, load ESM through require(esm) on Node 26, which adds risk for no benefit. `units/PLT-02-contracts-package-platform-slice.md` line 20 allows either format if both runtimes load it, so CJS is a permitted choice, not a contradiction.
- **Reason (Navigator):** The draft already emits CJS. The Angular consequence is additive (an `allowedCommonJsDependencies` entry can be added later), so this is a low-risk one-way door. PLT-03 names no output-format constraint.
- **Driver note:** Recorded now, before the plans are revised, because driver rule 5 requires each decision to be recorded and committed at once.

## 2026-11-11 — PLT-10 — criterion 3 method: local PR-title script (Navigator decision)

The header pattern is the one in `plans/PLT-10.md` section 2. The title reaches the script only through the environment variable `PR_TITLE`, never inside a `run:` line, so the title cannot inject shell code.

## 2026-11-11 — PLT-09 — rule implementation: eslint-plugin-boundaries, with a named fallback (Navigator decision)

- **Decision (Navigator):** Option A. The boundary rule is `eslint-plugin-boundaries` 7.4.2, as a new exact root devDependency, as `plans/PLT-09.md` section 1 describes. Unit line 21 permits it. The plugin has the primitives the rules need: element types declared with a glob and a capture list (for the module slug), and ordered policies that allow or forbid element combinations.
- **Two required refinements, recorded:**
  1. **Rule name.** The draft's name `boundaries/dependencies` (plans/PLT-09.md lines 29, 39 and 68) is stale. The plugin's v2 migration guide renames the rule to `boundaries/element-types`. The v7 name is not confirmed by the search, so step 4 confirms it against the installed v7 docs. The config, the criteria and the file list then use that name.
  2. **Fallback, named in advance.** If step 4 finds that the plugin cannot express rules R1 to R3, the fallback is Option B: a local ESLint rule that resolves each import with a module resolver (`eslint-import-resolver-node`, which the plugin already brings) and checks the module and feature rules. The switch is recorded in this file when it happens. It is not silent.
- **Rejected (Navigator):** Option C, `no-restricted-imports` patterns alone, because the patterns match the raw import string and cannot see the file a relative import resolves to.
- **Residual risk (not a blocker):** how an entry file (`*.module.ts`) is told apart from an internal file, and the base for path matching (cwd or config directory), are unconfirmed. The step 4 stop rule covers both.
- **No criterion text changes.** The unit's Decisions line 21 is an implementation choice, not a criterion.
- **Driver note:** Recorded now, before the plan is revised, because driver rule 5 requires each decision to be recorded and committed at once.

## 2026-11-12 — PLT-04 — Opus plan review of revision 3: APPROVED (point 1 still open)

- **Result (Opus subagent, fresh context, review of `plans/PLT-04.md` revision 3):** APPROVED. All eight revision-3 changes are correct. - **Factual correction (non-blocking, to apply in revision 4):** the plan says the `abortOnError` default "prints nothing". That is wrong. Nest's exception handler logs the error and its stack through the app logger, then calls `process.exit(1)`. With `abortOnError: false`, the error is logged and `create` rejects. Also, a rejected `onApplicationBootstrap` makes `init()` reject in both modes; the option matters only for errors while providers are built.

Line 131 must say both `SCRIBA_LIBRARY` cases use the unwritable-`.scriba` setup. `SCRIBA_LIBRARY=""` fails the non-empty rule, so PLT-15 must not pass an empty value.

## 2026-11-12 — PLT-04 — open point 1: CommonJS for apps/api (Navigator decision, disposition of the PLT-04 plan's open point 1)

- **Decision (Navigator, session `d057c67d2d0a4138867903c9519fad6c`):** Option A, CommonJS. `apps/api/package.json` has no `"type"` field. `tsconfig.base.json` keeps `module` and `moduleResolution` NodeNext, so tsc emits CJS. `__dirname` is used in `bootstrap.ts` and `create-test-app.ts`. The ESM-only NestJS 12 packages are loaded from CJS through `require`.
- **Evidence:** the scratch CJS build in `plans/PLT-04.md` section 7 (TypeScript 6.0.3, NodeNext) booted `@nestjs/core` 12.4.2 on Node v26.13.0 with no warning. Opus approved revisions 2 and 3 on this path. Search results (secondary sources, not opened) say Node 22.12.0 enables require(esm) by default and that it is marked stable from Node 25.4.0.
- **Rejected (Navigator):** Option B, ESM with `"type": "module"`. It needs `.js` suffixes on every relative import, `fileURLToPath` for `__dirname`, and Vitest and SWC config changes. Those changes reach PLT-05 to PLT-08 and give no benefit.
- **Downstream effect:** PLT-05 to PLT-08 write imports without `.js` suffixes and use `__dirname`. PLT-09's boundaries lint is unchanged. PLT-11 owns the Angular half, including any `allowedCommonJsDependencies` entry (line 173 of this file).
- **Driver note:** Open point 1 is settled by this entry. PLT-04 status stays `draft`. The Navigator's suggestion to set `planned` now was not taken: prompt.md step 5 sets `planned` only after Opus approves the plan text. Revision 5 was rejected (`af54076`), its corrections are applied, and they have not been re-reviewed.

## 2026-11-12 — PLT-06 — open point 4 (shutdown window): option (A), a known gap (driver decision, recorded before `planned`)

- **Situation:** a stream that opens after `beforeApplicationShutdown` runs and before dispose is not guarded, because `subscribe()` does not check the stopping flag. The code author found this at the PLT-06 revision 2 code stop. The heartbeat keeps such a stream active, so `server.close()` waits until the client leaves, and a SIGTERM exit can stall. Nothing in the plan's procedure covers it.
- **Decision (driver, the simplest option, option A):** leave it as a known gap of the shutdown window. Do not guard `subscribe()` in this unit.
- **Reason:** no acceptance criterion of `units/PLT-06-sse-stream-publisher.md` covers shutdown. The gap only opens for a stream opened after `beforeApplicationShutdown` has begun. Option B adds one check to the procedure and is a behaviour change to the publisher's public path; it is not needed for the criteria. A later unit or a shutdown test can add the guard.
- **Opus note, recorded:** the review of revision 3 (`a47be7f048b8f9020`, approved with four text items) asked for this decision to be recorded before `planned`. The text items are applied to `plans/PLT-06.md` on main.

## 2026-11-12 — PLT-12 — Opus plan review of revision 2 (`plans/PLT-12.md`, status draft): NOT APPROVED (rejection 2 of 3; one blocking item, six text items)

- **Blocking 1:** the wire format in decisions 23 and 24 omits `role` and the tool-call `type`. Without them the SDK's streaming helper (`chat.completions.stream(...).finalChatCompletion()`) fails ("missing role for choice 0", "tool call snapshot missing `type`"), so MPR-04 cannot use it against the mock. The SDK types `tool_calls[i]` as a union, so criterion 2's `.function.arguments` needs a `type === 'function'` check first. Correction: the first streamed delta carries `role: "assistant"`; each call's first chunk carries `index`, `id`, `type: "function"`, `function.name` and `function.arguments: ""`; the non-streamed reply has `message.role` and `type: "function"`; criterion 1 adds a `finalChatCompletion()` assertion on `tool-call`; criterion 2 checks `type` before reading `.function.arguments`.
- **Text 2:** step 3 should run a plain `npm install`, not an explicit `@types/node@^26.6.6` re-resolve, or state that the lock gate stops if a newer 26.x is published first.
- **Text 3:** section 3 should carry the revision-1 note "implement in a fresh worktree" (the main checkout's `node_modules` is from 2026-11-10 and has no `@types/node`).
- **Text 4:** step 12 should name the banner-count command, as PLT-11 criterion 2 does: `grep -o '^> @bbartolome/scriba-[a-z0-9]*' | sort -u | wc -l`.
- **Text 5:** no test covers the `header`, `turnIndex`, `lastMessageRole`/`lastMessageContent` matchers or `chunkDelayMs`, though MPR-04, AGL-08 and AGL-16 rely on them. Add a small test for each and define `lastMessageContent` for array content parts.
- **Text 6:** section 7 cites `core/error.d.ts` line 8 as `Headers`; the file says `readonly headers: THeaders` (generic).
- **Text 7:** section 5 should record that MPR-04 pins `openai` 7.55.0 in apps/api, so the repository keeps one copy.

## 2026-11-12 — PLT-15 — Opus plan review of revision 3 (`plans/PLT-15.md`, status draft): NOT APPROVED (rejection 3 of 3; disagreement sent to the Navigator)

- **Result (Opus subagent, fresh context):** the revision-2 items and the non-blocking item are fixed (step 0 guard, step 9c hand-off, criterion 2 wording and Mac `ls -ln`, `:ro` socket mount with the kernel-source reason, Dockerfile lines 60/63/64, PLT-08 filter credit). New items:
  1. **Disagreement (for the Navigator):** criterion 2 expects owner values that Docker Desktop's file sharing does not show. The shared-folder view reports each file as owned by the reader: busybox run as 1001:1001, 4242:4343 and 0:0 each see different owners for the same root-written file; from the shell it reads 1000:1000; back-to-back calls return the previous reader's cached owner. So `ls -ldn` prints 1000 1000 and root busybox `stat` prints 0:0, whatever the app does. Section 4's "proves the UID:GID the container gives the files it writes" is false on this daemon. Proposed correction: on Docker Desktop, evidence is `id -u`/`id -g` = 1001 plus `.scriba/` being created (proves the process UID and the successful write, not the owner); the literal `stat -c %u:%g` = 1001:1001 check stays on the native Linux leg; the Mac `ls -ln` is recorded only.
  2. `$INST`, `$LIB` and `cd "$INST"` do not persist between tool calls. If `$INST` is empty, `cd ""` succeeds and `docker compose down -v` runs in the current folder. Correction: print and record the two `mktemp` paths at step 9; write later commands with literal paths or `${INST:?}` / `${LIB:?}` guards (for example `docker compose --project-directory "${INST:?}" …`); re-run the step-0 guard before `up`; before `down -v`, confirm `docker compose ls -a` shows `scriba` with config file `$INST/compose.yaml`. Or run the whole check under `-p plt15-check`.
  3. Step 12's cleanup mounts the whole workspace read-write into a root container to `rm -rf` globs; too wide. Correction: `docker run --rm -v "${LIB:?}":/l busybox rm -rf /l/.scriba`, then `rm -rf -- "${LIB:?}" "${INST:?}"` from the shell; if refused, record the paths in step 11 as PLT-14 did.
  4. Step 8 retags `bbartolome/scriba:local-arm64`, which is the PLT-14 image (`a5fca3e65422`, whose criteria 1 and 5 are still with the Navigator), so the PLT-14 image becomes untagged. Correction: build under `SCRIBA_IMAGE_TAG=plt15-check`, remove it in step 12, adjust section 5; or state that both images are left behind on purpose.
- **Non-blocking:** section 7 fact 2 still names `54b469c` as HEAD (it is `ceae8d9`); fact 14 cites `sb_permission`, which covers read-only filesystems, not a `:ro` bind mount (the conclusion stands); add `sleep 1` after `python3 -m http.server` in the Mac recipe.
- **Assumed by the reviewer:** `host.docker.internal:18080` reaches the Mac's loopback publish; a `.env` file resolves the bare key to "1"; a connect through the `:ro` mount works at run time; UID 1001 can enter the socket folder; the Mac `ls -ln` shows the Mac user; `ipconfig` and `python3` exist on the Mac; `id` and `printenv` exist in the runtime image; `env_file` with `required` needs Compose v2.24 or later.

## 2026-11-13 — PLT-15 — Navigator decision on the criterion 2 owner check (after Opus plan rejection 3 of 3)

- **Decision A (Navigator, option b):** criterion 2 ("files the app writes into the library are owned by `SCRIBA_UID:SCRIBA_GID` on the host") stays unticked and is marked cannot-test-locally. The unit is complete without the literal host-owner check (prompt.md, "Criteria that you cannot test").
- **Decision B (Navigator):** the process-UID proof (`id -u` and `id -g` = 1001 in the `scriba` container, and `.scriba/` created by that process) is supporting evidence that `user:` is applied. It is not a substitute for the host-owner check. The criterion wording is unchanged.
- **Native Linux leg (open point 3):** optional, not required before status `done`. Recorded as not run until it runs; when it runs it records `stat -c %u:%g` on the host path, where "owned on the host" is exact.
- **Reason:** Docker Desktop's file sharing (VirtioFS) reports an owner that is not the creator's UID and changes between reads, so an owner check cannot verify the host owner on this daemon. Driver probe, 2026-11-13, a root-created file in a temp folder under the workspace: root `0:0`; UID 1001 `0:0`; UID 4242 `0:0`; the shell `0:0`; the shell again after 5 s `1000:1000`; root again `1000:1000`. The Navigator's step a1 wording "the reader's UID" does not match this probe, and the plan uses the corrected wording.
- **Plan changes (`plans/PLT-15.md`, revision 5, still untracked draft):** the `[PENDING NAVIGATOR: owner check]` markers are replaced by this decision. The criterion 2 test is the `id -u`, `id -g` and `.scriba/` check; `ls -ldn` and busybox `stat` are removed from it. The step 9c Mac `ls -ln` stays as a recorded observation. Open points 3 and 6 are resolved.

So the DNS check does not show that `extra_hosts` on `tailscale` reaches `scriba`, and section 8's "Criterion 4 checks it" is wrong. Correction: add `docker compose -p plt15-check --project-directory "${INST:?}" exec -T scriba grep host.docker.internal /etc/hosts` expecting one `host-gateway` line; note that the DNS lookup alone passes either way on Docker Desktop; fix section 8.
  6. Cleanup: step 4's `/tmp` copies of `deploy/` are never named or removed. Correction: `mktemp -d /tmp/plt15-cfg.XXXX` in step 4, removed in step 12. Step 0 adds `docker image ls -q bbartolome/scriba:plt15-check` printing nothing (true today).
  7. Non-blocking: section 1 should source "pass `SCRIBA_LIBRARY` to the app" to the PLT-04 hand-off (DECISIONS line 413, "PLT-04 — Opus plan review of revision 2"), not only the empty-value rule.

## 2026-11-13 — PLT-16 — Finding during the smoke run: the late-login premise of ST-1 fails under the shipped compose (evidence; not a decision)

- **Evidence (Opus code review of `c75948c`, reproduced by the coordinator with read-only commands on the `plt16-check` stack):** with no auth key, containerboot's `tailscale up` times out after about 60 s and the sidecar exits. Docker restarts it: `RestartCount` 8 at 05:07 UTC. Each restart prints a new login URL, so a URL handed over at step 11 is replaced within about a minute. The sidecar's log shows the new `AuthURL` at each start (the URL itself is not recorded here).
- **Effect on `scriba`:** `scriba` runs with `network_mode: service:tailscale`, and it was never restarted (`StartedAt` 04:59:01 UTC). Its network namespace belongs to the earlier sidecar instance. At 05:07 UTC the host `/healthz` on port 18080 returns 000. Inside `scriba`, the route table is empty and the `host.docker.internal` fetch fails with ENETUNREACH, while `scriba`'s own healthcheck still reports healthy.
- **What this means for the plan:** ST-1 (unit line 21) says "start compose with no auth key, app healthy on loopback; log in later via the auth URL". Under the shipped compose, a later login cannot reach Serve. The served app is unreachable after any restart of the sidecar, and a person cannot approve a URL that is gone within a minute. The N1 approval step is therefore not achievable as the plan describes it.
- **Steps 7 to 9 stay valid:** they ran in the first window (steps 7 and 8 between 04:59:01 and 05:00:01 UTC; step 8 at 04:59:27 to 04:59:38). Step 9's exact time is not logged.
- **Status:** the batch merge of PLT-16 waits for the Navigator's decision on this finding. Criteria 1 to 4 stay unticked. The stack is kept for the Navigator's check.

## 2026-11-13 — PLT-16 — Navigator decision on the late-login finding (8b3dec6): option (a), recorded as a FAILURE (Navigator session 43e5a7b0)

- **Decision:** the ST-1 late-login half is recorded as FAILED under the shipped compose. It is a failure, not a deferral: the flow was run and broke. Criterion 1 (unit line 27) stays unticked, with the cause. The PLT-16 batch waits. The compose fix is a separate change (a PLT-15 revision or a new unit). Merging PLT-16 without criterion 1 is a separate later decision. Do not merge now.
- **Cause (reproduced by the coordinator, read-only):** with no auth key, `tailscale up` times out after about 60 s and the sidecar exits; Docker restarts it (RestartCount 8), and each restart prints a new login URL, so a URL handed over at step 11 is dead within about a minute. `scriba` (`network_mode: service:tailscale`) was never restarted, so its namespace belongs to the old sidecar instance, and host `/healthz` on port 18080 returns 000. Both contradict unit line 21: "log in later via the auth URL … still does after `docker compose restart`".
- **Why not (b) or (c):** (b) changes the compose file, which is PLT-15's approved deliverable (reopening it as a side effect of a smoke test would conflate verification with a product change). (c) changes what criterion 1 tests, which only the Navigator may do, and it would weaken the criterion (prompt.md line 146).
- **Consequences:** the ADR's Attempt A records the late-login failure with this evidence, and its `Shipped variant` line stays open until the compose fix is re-tested. Criteria 2 to 4 keep the earlier decisions N2 to N5 (N2 reboot deferred; N3 Linux leg deferred; N5 2c deferred). N1 (the person's login approval) cannot complete while the URL is ephemeral.
- **Downstream effect:** `phase:smoke` (PLT-16) stays open, so PLT-17 and SAU-21 stay blocked (unit lines 9 and 14 of PLT-16).

## 2026-11-13 — SHL-01 / SHL-02 / SHL-03 — zod in the browser bundle: the Shell ships no runtime zod (Navigator decision, option c; session 17b006e4a2bb4441868aca77bff75496)

- **Decision (Navigator):** the Shell (SHL-02, SHL-03, SHL-04) ships no runtime zod. All contracts imports in the Shell are type-only, and type imports are erased at build. Runtime validation uses hand-written guards: `isPlatformErrorBody` for error bodies (SHL-02, decision 6) and an `isPlatformEnvelope` guard for the SSE envelope (SHL-03). A Vitest parity test runs each guard against the contracts schemas in Node, where the CommonJS build is fine.
- **No budget change:** `apps/web/angular.json` stays as it is (warning 500 kB, error 1 MB). The default build keeps no `allowedCommonJsDependencies` entry. Only `contracts-boot` has one (line 55).
- **SHL-08:** its runtime setup-auth schema is loaded by SHL-08 itself: a dynamic import in its own chunk, or a dedicated build configuration with `allowedCommonJsDependencies` as `contracts-boot` has. The Shell batch does not preload it. SHL-08's plan decides this when it is written.
- **Reasons:** (1) consistency: SHL-02 decision 5 already took this route, and PLT-11 (DECISIONS line 631) kept contracts and zod out of the production bundle. (2) Cost: a runtime import of `/platform` adds about 564 kB of zod's CommonJS build (DECISIONS line 634); the initial bundle would be about 886 kB, above the 500 kB warning. (3) A dynamic import (SHL-03 option a) only moves the 564 kB to a startup chunk, and the default build still lacks the CommonJS allowance. (4) Raising the budget is a one-way change that every later feature inherits.
- **Consequences for the drafts:** `plans/SHL-03.md` open point 1 (line 175) flips from the static import to this route. Its eager `/platform` import becomes `import type`, with the hand-written `isPlatformEnvelope` guard and the parity test; open point 6 (CommonJS warnings) is closed, because the default build needs no allowance. `plans/SHL-02.md` open point 1 (line 143) is closed by this entry; decision 5 is unchanged.
- **Note:** SHL-01's open point 1 ("for the Navigator, before SHL-02") is answered by this entry.

## 2026-11-13 — SHL-03 — Opus code review of `unit/SHL-03` (head 402e9bc, base main 3345bfe): NOT APPROVED (rejection 1 of the code)

- **Result (Opus subagent, fresh context):** NOT APPROVED. One blocking problem, and the fix stays inside the plan's files and the text of decision 9.
  1. [blocking] Criterion 3 and decision 9: the backoff does not grow when a recovered stream closes again before it opens. `recover()` in `sse-client.ts` adds to `failures` only when a probe fails or returns 401; when both probes pass it calls `connect('reconnecting')` and adds nothing. If that stream reaches CLOSED without opening, the next wait uses the same n. The reviewer's scratch spec gave EventSources at 0, 1000, 2000, 3000, 4000, 5000 and 6000 ms: a fixed 1 s loop of three requests per cycle (healthz, status, events) that never reaches the 30 s cap. Decision 9 counts "failed recoveries since the last open", so a recovery whose stream never opened is a failed one. Correction: in `recover()`, add `this.failures += 1;` just before `this.connect('reconnecting')` (`handleOpen` already resets it to 0). Make T9 stricter in place (still 12 tests): after the second stream at 1 s, fire `emitError(2)` on it, advance 2 s, and assert that the third EventSource has `createdAt` 3000. The reviewer's scratch probe gives 0, 1000, 3000 with this fix, and all 12 tests pass.

## 2026-11-13 — SHL-06 — criterion 1 on the dev stack: option (b), a dev-only proxy hook (Navigator decision; session 57d851d74d62450ca9f7cab2d9b775ec)

- **Decision (Navigator, option b):** a dev-proxy `configure` hook destroys the client response when the upstream aborts, so the browser's EventSource fires its error and the existing CONNECTING path runs on the dev stack. Criterion 1's wording (unit line 19) stands. Its verify note: the dev stack through the proxy hook, plus a one-time production stop check on the API-served build (single origin, native EventSource error).
- **Facts (checked):** the Vite 8.3.7 proxy pipes the upstream response into the client response with no abort handling (`node_modules/vite/dist/node/chunks/node.js` line 19252), so after the API destroys the stream on shutdown (`apps/api/src/modules/platform/events/events.publisher.ts` lines 70 to 77) the browser's stream stays half-open. Production has no proxy: NestJS serves the bundle on one origin, and a process stop closes the TCP connection, so the EventSource error fires natively.
- **Rejected (a):** a heartbeat watchdog in `SseClient`. The 15 s heartbeat is a comment line (`events.publisher.ts` line 56), which the browser's EventSource does not expose; an envelope-only watchdog would reconnect a healthy quiet stream every ~30 s, against the "small, unobtrusive" indicator (unit line 15). A data-line heartbeat is a Platform protocol change (PLT-06): a one-way door, disproportionate to a dev-demo gap.
- **Rejected (c):** judging criterion 1 on the API-served build only. SHL-09's demo step 5 needs the same behaviour on `npm run dev`.
- **Consequence for the files:** the hook is dev-only local code with no external request, so PLT-03's offline rationale is unchanged. It needs a proxy config that can carry a hook: the change is for the SHL-06 plan (to be revised), not for PLT-03's merged code by itself.

## 2026-11-13 — SHL-05 — gated URL: the larger-screen page renders in place (Navigator decision; session 57d851d74d62450ca9f7cab2d9b775ec)

- **Decision (Navigator, option a):** a phone that opens a gated URL keeps the URL it asked for, and the "needs a larger screen" page renders there. The gate's `canMatch` returns false on a phone, so the gated route does not match and a fallback renders at the same URL. The page has an in-page link back to a useful destination (home or the project list). The browser back button returns to the previous page, with no redirect loop. The draft's redirect to `/larger-screen` (`plans/SHL-05.md` decision 7) is replaced by this.
- **Reason:** the unit (`units/SHL-05-breakpoint-gate-nav.md` line 19) says "opening it directly shows a 'needs a larger screen' page with a link back"; criterion line 25 says "opening its URL on a phone viewport shows the 'larger screen' page". Neither requires a URL change. `canMatch` returning false is the idiomatic reading of `modules/shell.md` line 63. A redirect would make the back button loop (back, gated URL, redirect, back). Preserving the URL also means a rotation or resize up shows the real page without a second navigation.
- **Constraint for the design (added here):** the fallback must not catch unknown URLs, which keep the NotFound page (SHL-01 decision 8). The fallback is therefore one generated sibling per gated entry, with the same path and a `canMatch` that is true only when the device is below the entry's minimum; this is generated by `withDeviceGate`, not written into the route table.
- **Correction to a citation in the entry above (d2a3a4f):** the AGL-07 docked-slot wording is at `units/AGL-07-chat-panel-ui.md` line 17 (its first Decisions bullet), not line 8 (line 8 is `parallel_with`).

## 2026-11-13 — SAU-03 — Opus plan review of revision 1 (`plans/SAU-03.md`, draft): NOT APPROVED (rejection 1)

  1. Decision 20 cannot produce criterion 2's page. In Nest 12.4.2 (npm cache), a handler with `@Redirect` always redirects: `@Redirect()` sets the URL to '' by default, and the redirect test (`router-execution-context.js` line 253) is true for any string URL, so a wrong token gets a 302, not the page. With `@Res({passthrough: true})`, `reply()` resets the status to the `@HttpCode` value or 200 (`express-adapter.js` lines 90 to 92), so `res.status(401)` is lost. Correction: the handshake handler takes `@Res()` without passthrough, typed structurally, and writes either the 303 (Location, Set-Cookie, Referrer-Policy) or the 401 HTML page itself; it uses no `@Redirect`. Record as FACT in section 7(a).
  2. [Navigator] Criterion 6 is handed to "the owner" (lines 118 and 144). No person answers (prompt.md line 1); prompt.md lines 148 to 153 give the decision to the Navigator. The Playwright substitute checks only the cookie the app issues; it must also set a probe cookie with `Secure` over http on each Host and record, per engine, whether it is stored and then sent; and the results are Linux Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, not macOS browsers. [Navigator, with SAU-03 and SAU-05] Decision 20, row 8 and open point 1: `sessionCookie` rebuilds the cookie from `setup-token` by copying `SetupCookieSigner`'s internals (KEY_LABEL and the double HMAC). That puts the signing helper into the test, which `modules/setup-auth.md` line 50 forbids, and DECISIONS line 1434 says SAU-04 covers `verify` over HTTP. ## 2026-11-13 — SAU-04 — Revision 2 addition to `plans/SAU-04.md`: malformed `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS` refused at boot (decision 7)

- **Addition the unit does not ask for:** `units/SAU-04-global-guard.md` does not require a check on `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS`. Revision 2 of the plan keeps one. Each entry of `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS` must be an `http:` or `https:` origin with an explicit port and no path, user or query. A bad entry makes `RequestPolicy.onApplicationBootstrap` throw a `BootError` (`Invalid SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS "<raw>": ...`).
- **Reason:** an unchecked entry would silently never match a Host or Origin, so the dev stack would refuse the browser with no message. The refusal is at boot, so it fails fast and the same way as the settings refusal.
- **Test:** the extra `it` of `apps/api/test/setup-auth-guard.test.ts` spawns `dist/main.js` (as `env-boot.test.ts` and `settings-refusal.test.ts` do) with `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS=localhost:4200` and `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS=http://localhost`. Expected for each: exit code 1, empty stdout, and a stderr line naming `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS`.
- **Status:** an addition, not a unit requirement. The coordinator may drop it at implementation review. The plan's mutation M10 removes the check to confirm the test fails.

## 2026-11-13 — SAU-04 — Navigator decision: the Host check and CSP run before the body parser (revision 1 rejection, items 2 and 5)

- **Decision (Navigator, session `7bd39660cbee4380bf275ec4c4e1de9d`, answer to the parser question):** the Host check and the `Content-Security-Policy` header run before the body parser. Mechanism a1: `app.use(...)` in `apps/api/src/bootstrap.ts` between `NestFactory.create` and `app.init()`. Scope i: registered only when `SetupAuthModule` is in the composed module list, so the ten Platform-only test apps (modules `[PlatformModule]`) do not change. The refusal (403 `AUTH_HOST_REJECTED`) and the CSP are written by the middleware itself, in Platform's base error shape.
- **Reason:** the body parser is registered in `NestApplication.init()` (`@nestjs/core` 12.4.2, `package/nest-application.js` line 127), not in `NestFactory.create`, so middleware added before `init()` runs first. A POST with a foreign Host and a malformed or oversize body then gets 403 `AUTH_HOST_REJECTED` with the CSP, not 400 or 413. Production always composes `SetupAuthModule`, so the security posture is the same under scope i and scope ii.
- **Corrections by the driver to the first answer:** that answer said the placement is a provider or an `httpServer` option. Both are wrong: `NestFactory.create` takes the adapter as its second argument and has no `httpServer` option (`package/nest-factory.js` lines 27 to 30); a provider's `onModuleInit` runs inside `init()`, after the parser; and the Platform test apps are not unaffected, because `createTestApp` listens on an ephemeral port (`apps/api/test/create-test-app.ts` line 57) that the allowlist refuses. These were recorded as rejected claims in the Navigator session.
- **Open for the plan (not decided here):** where the `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS` validation runs, since the Host map is built before `app.use`. It must still refuse at boot with `BootError` (exit 1, a stderr line naming `SCRIBA_DEV_ORIGINS`). The planner decides, and records the decision in the plan.
- **Consequences for the SAU-04 plan (revision 3):** decisions 1, 11 and 15 and criteria 2 and 5 change. The Host check and the CSP are no longer Nest middleware. Decision 11 (errors from middleware reach Platform's filter) does not hold for an express-level middleware and must be checked before the plan says so.

## 2026-11-13 — SAU-04 — Opus plan review of revision 5 (`plans/SAU-04.md`, draft): NOT APPROVED (rejection 4; carries a design point)

- **Result (Opus subagent, fresh context):** NOT APPROVED. One design point goes back to the Navigator; the rest are text.
  1. [DESIGN POINT] Criterion 5 fails for a response the shipped app sends, and no test can see it. The bundle has a `media/` folder: `apps/web/src/styles.css` line 2 imports `@fontsource/material-icons/index.css`, and `@angular/build` 22.2.5 writes fonts referenced from CSS to `media/` (`src/builders/application/schema.json` line 343; `options.js` line 170). The Dockerfile copies that folder (line 45), and it is the default static root (`bootstrap.ts` lines 16 to 24). So `GET /media` reaches `express.static` with the default `redirect: true`: `app.module.ts` (lines 37 to 40) passes no `serveStaticOptions`, and serve-static 2.2.1 answers 301 with `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'` (`index.js` line 203). That replaces the hook's `frame-ancestors 'none'`. The fixture `apps/api/test/fixtures/static` has no subdirectory, so the test cannot see it. Practical risk is low (a browser does not render a redirect in a frame, and `/media/` falls through to the SPA with the hook's CSP). Options: (a) `serveStaticOptions: { redirect: false }` in `app.module.ts` (a Platform file: add a plan row, a fixture subdirectory, `GET /media` in criterion 5, and a mutation that restores the redirect); (b) accept the exception for directory redirects, record it in criterion 5 and in open point 10.

## 2026-11-13 — SAU-04 — Navigator decision on the design point of revision 5: `redirect: false` for the static root (option a)

- **Decision (Navigator, session `7bd39660cbee4380bf275ec4c4e1de9d`):** add `serveStaticOptions: { redirect: false }` to the `ServeStaticModule.forRoot` call in `apps/api/src/app.module.ts`. Add a `media/` subdirectory to the static fixture; add `GET /media` to criterion 5; add a mutation that restores the default redirect and must fail criterion 5. Then the driver writes revision 6 with the text fixes of the revision 4 and revision 5 reviews, and a fresh Opus review follows.
- **Reason:** unit line 24 requires the hook's CSP on every response, including static. The default redirect answers `GET /media` with a 301 whose `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'` replaces the hook's `frame-ancestors 'none'` (`@nestjs/serve-static` 2.2.1 `index.js` line 203). An exception would make the invariant conditional for every later static change. A one-property change makes it total. The Platform file is in scope because it serves the guard's own invariant.
- **Driver note:** the excerpt in the question (`app.module.ts` lines 36 to 41) was off: the block is lines 36 to 40, and the indentation differed. The substance was right. The correction is recorded in the Navigator session.
- **Consequences:** `app.module.ts` is a changed file of this unit (a row in section 2); `Not changed` no longer lists it.

## 2026-11-13 — SAU-07 — Opus plan review of revision 3 (`plans/SAU-07.md`, draft): NOT APPROVED (rejection 3)

- **Result (Opus subagent, fresh context):** NOT APPROVED. Four items.
  1. [blocking; test mechanics] Tests 1, N1 and N4 hang on `HEAD /events`, so they fail at their 60000 ms timeout on main. `events.controller.ts` lines 11 to 18 write the stream headers and never end the response; Node sends headers only at `end()`, and `write()` on a HEAD response is ignored. Router 2.2.0 sends HEAD to the GET handler (`lib/route.js` lines 64 and 111). A read-only `node -e` check on v26.13.0 confirmed it: GET got 200 `text/event-stream` in 5 ms, HEAD got no response after 3000 ms. `send` in `guard-request.ts` (lines 38 to 60) resolves only on the response callback, with no timeout. Correction: each sweep request resolves at its response headers, or after a fixed cap with no headers (a named constant, for example 1000 ms); its socket is destroyed and it counts as sent; no status is asserted. `invariant-sweep.ts` gets its own `node:http` sender, or `send` gets the option and row 1 changes. Decision 13 and the header comment say that `HEAD /events` is such a request on main. The cap enters the cost formula (row 3) and step 9; the `node -e` check goes in 7(a). `events.controller.ts` is Platform's file and is not changed.
  2. [TEXT] Decision 8 and decision 13 disagree on the final comparison. Decision 8 says one snapshot before the first request and one after each request, plus a final one, and compares the first with the last; decision 13 step 3 compares `first` with `current` (the last after-snapshot), which cannot add an entry, and a write after the last snapshot is never seen. Correction: after the final drain take `last := snapshotLibrary(library)`; if `last` differs from `current`, add `library-changed` for the previous request when it has no entry; then compare `first` with `last`.
  3. [TEXT] Citations point at headings: decision 8 cites DECISIONS line 1515 for `ino` and `ctimeMs` (the text is at 1517); 7(a) cites 1504 for the `parallel_with` correction (it is at 1507); open point 8 cites 1504 for module line 50 (it is at 1509 and 1512).
  4. [TEXT] Decision 11 says "ASSUMED for the POST-based reading of `ALL`", but the rule sends four methods and its reason is FACT: say "the four-method reading of `ALL`". 7(a) says the `apps/api/package.json` dependencies are at lines 10 to 19; they run from 10 to 20 (decision 17 says 10 to 25).
- **Checked and correct (reviewer):** the revision 2 items 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7 are applied (the ancestry and path-diff checks exit 0; M4 is named in test 2 and N2 and N3; `ALL` is sent as POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE, with `node -e` results ALL → 400 and POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE → 200; `router-explorer.js` lines 126 and 181; the 60000 ms timeouts; `platform.module.ts` line 39 has no `@Global`).
- **Criteria (reviewer):** 1 NOT COVERED as written (test 1 hangs on `HEAD /events`); it is covered once the sweep caps the header wait. 2 NOT COVERED as written for N1 and N4 (the same hang); N2 is sound.
- **Process:** this is the third rejection of SAU-07. Per `prompt.md` step 5, the disagreement goes to the Navigator (as it did for SAU-04).

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-01 — Navigator decision on open point 5 (custom-provider id base): option (a), an optional `label` in the create body, not stored (Navigator session 7a139b87f47f4e1b85168b19a9099fdf)

- **Decision (Navigator):** the create body gets an optional non-empty `label` field (SD3 of `plans/MPR-01.md`). It is not stored, like `presetId`. The id base is `presetId` when given, else `slug(label)`, normalized to the SD4 rule `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$`, with a suffix if taken. MPR-02's create rule (`units/MPR-02-registry-seeding.md` line 23) names this slug source. Its acceptance (line 29: two providers from one preset get distinct stable ids) is unchanged.
- **Rejected:** (b) a slug of the `baseURL` host, because two endpoints on one host collide, and the id would come from a mutable field. (c) a client-chosen required `id`, because a client could create `ollama`, and the suffix rule would overwrite the client's own id.
- **Why now:** `providerCreateSchema` is unpublished (phase:contract), so a one-field addition is cheap now. Deferring would leave MPR-02's acceptance without a slug source.
- **Driver's checks:** `plans/MPR-01.md` line 58 (SD3: the create body has no label and no id field today); `units/MPR-01-contract-slice.md` line 18 (the id bullet; the Navigator's citation of line 14 was wrong); `units/MPR-02-registry-seeding.md` lines 23 and 29.
- **Process:** the plan changes are text only, plus one unit line. They go to the planner, then to a targeted Opus review, before the MPR-01 code adds the field and step 13 commits.

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-01 — Coordinator decisions D-a, D-b and D-c on the create body (the gaps Opus found after the Navigator's decision c1d4804)

- **D-a (at least one id source):** `providerCreateSchema` is refined so that the body carries `presetId` or `label`, or both. A body with neither is refused. Without this, a valid body had no id base (Opus review, DECISIONS entry "targeted review of the label change").
- **D-b (preset ids only):** `presetId` is `z.enum(PROVIDER_PRESET_IDS)`, where `PROVIDER_PRESET_IDS` is the tuple `ollama`, `lm-studio`, `openai`, `openrouter`. So `presetId: '!!!'` is refused. Before this, any non-empty string passed, and it could slug to an empty base.
- **D-c (ASCII slug rule):** the label is lowercased first. Then each run of characters other than a–z and 0–9 becomes one hyphen, and leading and trailing hyphens are trimmed. The label must contain at least one ASCII letter or digit (`/[A-Za-z0-9]/`), so the slug is never empty. Examples: "My Local Llama" gives `my-local-llama`; "Café 2" gives `caf-2`; "日本語 1" gives `1`; "!!!" is refused.
- **Why coordinator decisions:** each is a small choice that the unit and module do not settle, and each only tightens the Navigator's option (a) (c1d4804). None changes the Navigator's rule that the label is optional and not stored, and the id base is `presetId`, else the slug of `label`.
- **Process:** the plan cites this entry next to c1d4804. The text goes through a targeted Opus review before `status: planned`.

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-04 — Navigator decision 2 of 3: a persistent 503 maps to PROVIDER_BAD_RESPONSE (option b), not PROVIDER_UNREACHABLE (Navigator session 4f72bf661d544ca681c339ebea1a4b61)

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-02 — coordinator decisions recorded for the draft (SD13 edge; the hook-order stop rule)

The edge: when Platform's hook has already written a backup and added a missing default (`setupAuth`), the seeder then refuses, and the file is not rolled back. The plan states this edge. ## 2026-11-13 — MPR-04 — coordinator decision on timeout 0, superseding DECISIONS line 1889 (entry "coordinator decisions on the drafts", f2260eb)

- **Supersedes line 1889.** Line 1889 says that a timeout of 0 runs no client timer and that the SDK's default bounds a silent wait. That is withdrawn. Its premise is wrong for chat streams: the SDK's timer is cleared once the headers arrive, and the built-in fetch (undici) caps a silent wait at 300 s.
- **Decision.** A `config.timeout` of 0 means a limit of **300 000 ms** (the client's own timer uses it, and the SDK receives the same value). A value above 300 000 is still enforced by the client's own timer, but undici ends a silent wait at 300 s first. The plan states that cap.
- **Errors.** Both undici timeout causes (`UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT`, `UND_ERR_BODY_TIMEOUT`) and the SDK's `APIConnectionTimeoutError` map to PROVIDER_TIMEOUT. An SDK connection error that is not a timeout maps to PROVIDER_UNREACHABLE, as before.
- **Rejected.** Keep 600 000 and record the cap (option a): the plan would then describe a limit that the runtime never gives. The 300 000 value makes the stated limit and the runtime agree.
- **Driver's checks:** the undici copy at 8.12.4, `lib/dispatcher/client.js` 318 and 319 (both 300 000 ms); the openai 7.55.0 copy (`client.js` 1824 is the 600 000 default; `APIConnectionTimeoutError` in `core/error.js` 78 and 88); Node 26.13.0 bundles undici 8.12.2.

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-04 — coordinator decision on a stream that ends normally after the first chunk (abort or timer)

- **Decision.** When the SDK's stream iterator ends normally after the first chunk, the client first checks the caller's abort (PROVIDER_ABORTED) and then its own timer (PROVIDER_TIMEOUT). Only if neither fired does the finish rule of S9 apply. Reason: the SDK returns silently on an abort (`core/streaming.js` lines 136 to 139 and 499 to 514), so without this check a cut-off stream would be reported as `finish stop`.
- **Accepted gap.** The path where the client's timer fires after the first chunk and the SDK iterator ends silently has no test: the mock's `chunkDelayMs` applies before every delta. It is named as a mock gap (MPR-04 open point 8). The timer before the first byte is tested (criterion 5, D11).
- **Driver's checks:** the SDK copy (openai 7.55.0, `core/streaming.js` 136 to 141 and 499 to 514). The stream-end rule is recorded in `plans/MPR-04.md` (lines 49, 50, 52, 207).

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-04 — coordinator decision widened: a stream that ends normally, with or without chunks (widens the entry at DECISIONS line 1915)

- **Decision.** When the SDK's stream iterator ends normally, with or without chunks, the client first checks the caller's abort (PROVIDER_ABORTED) and then its own timer (PROVIDER_TIMEOUT, retried only if no chunk had arrived). Only if neither fired does the rest apply: no chunk at all is PROVIDER_BAD_RESPONSE (not retried); otherwise the finish rule of S9 applies.
- **Reason.** The SDK's signal combination returns the iterator silently when the caller's signal aborts, even before the first chunk (`core/streaming.js` 499 to 514; the signal is combined after the headers, `client.js` 1878 to 1891). Without the check, a user Stop while the model is still thinking would be reported as BAD_RESPONSE.
- **Accepted gap.** The window between the headers and the first chunk is not reproducible with the mock (it writes the head and the first chunk together, `server.mjs` 408 to 422). It is named as a mock gap (MPR-04 open point 8).
- **Driver's checks:** the SDK copy (openai 7.55.0) `core/streaming.js` 136 to 141 and 499 to 514; `client.js` 1878 to 1891.

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-04 — coordinator decisions on the Opus code review of unit/MPR-04 (NOT APPROVED; the defects are fixed in the code, not the plan)

- **An empty key is no key.** `target.key` of `''` is treated as absent: a keyless provider uses the placeholder, and a keyRef without a key gives PROVIDER_NOT_CONFIGURED. Before, `''` reached the SDK constructor and escaped as an uncoded error (`openai-compatible-client.ts` lines 185 to 208). A test in D2 asserts it.
- **listModels validates the raw body.** A 200 reply that is not a model list (for example an HTML page from a wrong URL) is PROVIDER_BAD_RESPONSE, not an empty list. The SDK's page sets `data` to `[]` for any body, so the check reads the raw body (`client.get`) and requires `data` to be an array of objects with a string `id`. One test is added to the models file (D8b): a local server answers `text/html` with 200, and the client returns PROVIDER_BAD_RESPONSE. This adds one test to the 25 (the plan's section 4 count becomes 26).
- **Timer disarmed before a retry sleep.** The timer is disarmed (or the scope closed) before the backoff sleep, so a retry does not run under a stale timer.
- **Two tests tightened.** Criterion 6 asserts a single `events.next()` after the abort. D10 counts the events drained before the rejection and asserts at most one.
- **Commits.** The plan's step 15 names one commit; the code has four, one per part (the per-part rule). Accepted: the branch keeps four commits.
- **Driver's checks:** the Opus review's file and line citations (the empty-key path, the listModels guard at line 328, `core/pagination.js` 79 to 91); the SDK pagination lines read by the reviewer.

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-03 — coordinator decisions on the Opus code review (APPROVED, with two hardening notes)

- **Follow-up (not in this batch): a short stored key can hide an unknown token.** The redactor applies the known keys (rule a) before the Authorization rule (b) and the sk- rule (c). A stored key that is a piece of "Authorization", "Bearer", "Basic" or "sk-" can break those rules, so an unknown token on the same line may print. The risk is low: the app sends no request log, and the only Authorization header it sends carries the stored key, which rule (a) removes. A later change applies the three rules in one combined pass, with a test that stores the key "a". Recorded as a follow-up; not a blocker.
- **Accepted: Nest's function and class messages are not resolved.** The override inspects a function message (it prints `[Function ...]`) and a class message (it prints `[class ...]`), where Nest would call or name them. No caller in M1 passes one (the reviewer checked `apps/api/src`, `@nestjs/core` and `@nestjs/common`). This is a fourth difference from Nest's output, recorded in the plan's Platform row; a later change may call `resolveMessage` first.
- **Driver's checks:** the Opus review's file and line citations (`redact.ts` 33 to 40; `stdout-logger.ts` 51 to 76 and the function-message lines 61 to 65); Nest 12.4.2 `console-logger.service.js` 390 to 404 (resolveMessage and stringifyMessage).

## 2026-11-13 — MPR-05 — coordinator decisions on the second draft (the test route for C4; the bound on the model listing; the Ollama rule's export; a second provider for E2)

- **The test-only resolve route stays for C4 after MPR-06.** The chat-target test (C4) keeps the kit's test route after MPR-06 adds the real effective-model route; C1 to C3 and E1 to E3 repoint to the real routes. Source: the coordinator's message on this draft (not in the DECISIONS entry at 9c5db85; recorded here).
- **The bound on the model listing.** `auto` resolution calls `/v1/models` on every resolve, with no caller signal. It is bounded per attempt by the provider's timeout (120 000 ms by default; 0 means 300 000 ms), over up to `retries` + 1 attempts (2 retries by default), plus the backoff before each retry, with Retry-After uncapped. With the defaults, a silent server can hold one resolution for about six minutes. This is stated in the plan; AGL-03 decides how a Stop meets it.
- **The Ollama rule is exported through the module entry.** `isOllamaProvider` is re-exported from `providers.module.ts`, so E3 tests it through the module's API (the testing rule in `units/README.md` line 133).
- **E2 uses a second keyless provider.** A `models-failing` provider sits in the same `settings.yaml` as the `models-non-empty` one, so one boot reaches both scenarios.
- **Driver's checks:** `client/errors.ts` 114, 129 and 139; `retry.ts` 7 to 17 and 46 to 53; `defaults.ts` lines 8 and 9; the mock's `server.mjs` 279 to 302 and the scenario order (qwen2.5, gemma3, llama3.1).

Correction: `ids: z.union([entityIdSchema.transform((id) => [id]), z.array(entityIdSchema)])`, with tests: `{ids:'ann'}` parses to `['ann']`, `{ids:['ann','bob']}` passes, `{}` is refused.

D5 permits this, and D13 puts path rules in PST-02, whose path check works inside the project root, and `projectId` chooses that root. The alternative (amending D5 to refuse `/`, `\`, NUL and a leading dot) is not taken, because it changes a settled decision.
